Aiming at the centroid\n105\t    instead is tempting and wrong: a face that is L-shaped or has the rest of the part\n106\t    merged into it puts its centroid somewhere that has nothing to do with which side\n107\t    of this edge the material is on. That mistake showed up as convex slab edges\n108\t    reported concave.\n109\t    \"\"\"\n110\t    across = tangent.cross(face.normal_at(point)).normalized()\n111\t    forward = face.distance_to(point + across * PROBE)\n112\t    backward = face.distance_to(point - across * PROBE)\n113\t    return across if forward <= backward else -across\n114\t\n115\t\n116\tdef is_convex(edge, f1, f2):\n117\t    \"\"\"True if the material folds away from itself across this edge.\n118\t\n119\t    The test is `n1 . u2`: does the second face extend in the direction the first\n120\t    face's outward normal points? If it does, the solid is folding back on itself and\n121\t    the edge is concave.\n122\t\n123\t    The obvious alternative, offsetting the edge along the averaged face normals and\n124\t    asking whether that point is inside the solid, is recorded in the Fusion notes as\n125\t    failing. It cannot work: at a concave edge the averaged normal points into open\n126\t    space just as it does at a convex one, so both answer the same. What separates\n127\t    them is where the faces go, not where they face, which is why this uses a\n128\t    direction along the face and not the normal.\n129\t    \"\"\"\n130\t    point = edge.center()\n131\t    tangent = edge.tangent_at(0.5)\n132\t    return f1.normal_at(point).dot(_into_face(f2, point, tangent)) < 0\n133\t\n134\t\n135\tdef concave_edges(shape):\n136\t    \"\"\"Every concave edge, which is where polish is forbidden and stress collects.\"\"\"\n137\t    out = []\n138\t    for edge, faces in edge_faces(shape).items():\n139\t        if len(faces) != 2:\n140\t            continue  # seam or free edge, nothing to be convex about\n141\t        if not is_convex(edge, faces[0], faces[1]):\n142\t            out.append(edge)\n143\t    return out\n144\t\n145\t\n146\t# --- rules -------------------------------------------------------------------\n147\t\n148\t\n149\t@rule(\"sliver\")\n150\tdef sliver(shape, ctx):\n151\t    \"\"\"Faces too small to print as anything but a smear.\n152\t\n153\t    Chamfers meeting at a corner legitimately make a few, so a part declares how many\n154\t    it has earned. A count above the baseline is a regression; at or below it is\n155\t    silence. The count is the assertion, not the individual faces, because which face\n156\t    is which shifts as soon as anything upstream of the polish pass moves.\n157\t    \"\"\"\n158\t    small = [f for f in shape.faces() if f.area < ctx.sliver_area]\n159\t    allowed = ctx.accepted.get(\"sliver\", 0)\n160\t    if len(small) <= allowed:\n161\t        return []\n162\t    worst = min(small, key=lambda f: f.area)\n163\t    return [\n164\t        Finding(\n165\t            \"sliver\",\n166\t            WARN,\n167\t            f\"{len(small)} faces under {ctx.sliver_area}mm2, {allowed} accounted for\",\n168\t            value=round(worst.area, 3),\n169\t            where=tuple(round(v, 2) for v in worst.center()),\n170\t        )\n171\t    ]\n172\t\n173\t\n174\t@rule(\"build_volume\")\n175\tdef build_volume(shape, ctx):\n176\t    \"\"\"Does it fit on the printer at all.\"\"\"\n177\t    size = shape.bounding_box().size\n178\t    up = Vector(*ctx.up).normalized()\n179\t    if _standing(shape, up) is None:\n180\t        got = sorted([size.X, size.Y, size.Z], reverse=True)\n181\t        fits = all(g <= b for g, b in zip(got, sorted(ctx.bed, reverse=True)))\n182\t        if not fits:\n183\t            # The box is the wrong question for a part that fits turned on the\n184\t            # plate: issue #55's 364mm tray sits on a 350mm bed at 36 degrees.\n185\t            fits = _fits_rotated(shape, ctx.bed)\n186\t        orientation = \"in any orientation\"\n187\t    else:\n188\t        # A stance facet fixes the build direction. The part may turn on the plate,\n189\t        # but rolling it onto another axis would put the facet in the air again.\n190\t        bed, top = _span(shape, up)\n191\t        height = top - bed\n192\t        footprint = sorted(\n193\t            _span(shape, axis)[1] - _span(shape, axis)[0]\n194\t            for axis in _bed_axes(up)\n195\t        )\n196\t        plate = sorted(ctx.bed[:2])\n197\t        got = [height, *footprint]\n198\t        fits = height <= ctx.bed[2] and (\n199\t            all(got <= available for got, available in zip(footprint, plate))\n200\t            or _fits_rotated(shape, ctx.bed, up)\n201\t        )\n202\t        orientation = \"in its fixed build orientation\"\n203\t    if fits:\n204\t        return []\n205\t    return [\n206\t        Finding(\n207\t            \"build_volume\",\n208\t            FAIL,\n209\t            f\"{size.X:.0f} x {size.Y:.0f} x {size.Z:.0f}mm does not fit \"\n210\t            f\"{ctx.bed[0]:.0f} x {ctx.bed[1]:.0f} x {ctx.bed[2]:.0f}mm {orientation}\",\n211\t            value=round(max(got), 1),\n212\t        )\n213\t    ]\n214\t\n215\t\n216\tdef _sample_normals(face, grid=5):\n217\t    \"\"\"Outward normals across a face.\n218\t\n219\t    A planar face has one and it is exact. A curved face does not, and the sampling\n220\t    has to reach the ends of its parameter range: sampling cell midpoints instead\n221\t    walks a cylinder round at 45, 135, 225 and 315 degrees and never once looks\n222\t    straight down, which is the only normal an overhang check cares about. Endpoints\n223\t    included, the same grid hits 270 exactly.\n224\t\n225\t    Still an approximation on a curved face, bounded by the grid spacing. Planar\n226\t    faces, which is nearly all of this library, are exact.\n227\t    \"\"\"\n228\t    if face.geom_type == GeomType.PLANE:\n229\t        return [face.normal_at(face.center())]\n230\t    out = []\n231\t    for i in range(grid):\n232\t        for j in range(grid):\n233\t            try:\n234\t                spot = face.position_at(i / (grid - 1), j / (grid - 1))\n235\t                out.append(face.normal_at(spot))\n236\t            except Exception:\n237\t                continue\n238\t    return out or [face.normal_at(face.center())]\n239\t\n240\t\n241\tdef _span(shape, up):\n242\t    \"\"\"How far a shape reaches along the build direction, as (lowest, highest).\n243\t\n244\t    Off the bounding box, not off the vertices. A curved face has vertices only where\n245\t    its seam is, so a cylinder's lowest vertex sits at its middle: measuring from\n246\t    vertices put the bed 8mm above the actual bottom of a cylinder, marked every face\n247\t    grounded, and turned the overhang rule off without any sign that it had happened.\n248\t\n249\t    Exact for an axis-aligned build direction, which is what a part placed on a bed\n250\t    has. Conservative otherwise.\n251\t    \"\"\"\n252\t    box = shape.bounding_box()\n253\t    corners = [\n254\t        Vector(x, y, z)\n255\t        for x in (box.min.X, box.max.X)\n256\t        for y in (box.min.Y, box.max.Y)\n257\t        for z in (box.min.Z, box.max.Z)\n258\t    ]\n259\t    reach = [c.dot(up) for c in corners]\n260\t    return min(reach), max(reach)\n261\t\n262\t\n263\tdef _bed_axes(up):\n264\t    \"\"\"Two perpendicular directions across the build plate.\"\"\"\n265\t    guide = Vector(1, 0, 0) if abs(up.X) < 0.9 else Vector(0, 1, 0)\n266\t    first = up.cross(guide).normalized()\n267\t    return first, up.cross(first).normalized()\n268\t\n269\t\n270\tdef _hull(points):\n271\t    \"\"\"Convex hull of 2d points, by monotone chain.\"\"\"\n272\t    pts = sorted({(round(float(x), 2), round(float(y), 2)) for x, y in points})\n273\t    if len(pts) <= 2:\n274\t        return pts\n275\t\n276\t    def turn(o, a, b):\n277\t        return (a[0] - o[0]) * (b[1] - o[1]) - (a[1] - o[1]) * (b[0] - o[0])\n278\t\n279\t    out = []\n280\t    for chain in (pts, reversed(pts)):\n281\t        base = len(out)\n282\t        for p in chain:\n283\t            while len(out) - base >= 2 and turn(out[-2], out[-1], p) <= 0:\n284\t                out.pop()\n285\t            out.append(p)\n286\t        out.pop()\n287\t    return out\n288\t\n289\t\n290\tdef _rotated_spans(points, angle):\n291\t    c, s = cos(angle), sin(angle)\n292\t    xs = [x * c - y * s for x, y in points]\n293\t    ys = [x * s + y * c for x, y in points]\n294\t    return max(xs) - min(xs), max(ys) - min(ys)\n295\t\n296\t\n297\tdef _footprint_fits(points, plate):\n298\t    \"\"\"Whether a 2d footprint fits a rectangular plate at some rotation.\n299\t\n300\t    Between orientations parallel to hull edges, the vertices supporting each side\n301\t    of the bounding box stay fixed. The worst normalized span can reach its minimum\n302\t    only at one of those boundaries or where the two normalized spans cross, so those\n303\t    finite candidates give the exact answer without an angle grid.\n304\t    \"\"\"\n305\t    hull = _hull(points)\n306\t    if not hull:\n307\t        return False\n308\t\n309\t    quarter = pi / 2\n310\t    cuts = {0.0, quarter}\n311\t    if len(hull) > 1:\n312\t        for p, q in zip(hull, hull[1:] + hull[:1]):\n313\t            edge = atan2(q[1] - p[1], q[0] - p[0])\n314\t            cuts.add((-edge) % quarter)\n315\t    cuts = sorted(cuts)\n316\t    candidates = list(cuts)\n317\t    orientations = (plate, plate[::-1])\n318\t\n319\t    for low, high in zip(cuts, cuts[1:]):\n320\t        if high - low <= 1e-12:\n321\t            continue\n322\t        mid = (low + high) / 2\n323\t        c, s = cos(mid), sin(mid)\n324\t\n325\t        def x_at(p):\n326\t            return p[0] * c - p[1] * s\n327\t\n328\t        def y_at(p):\n329\t            return p[0] * s + p[1] * c\n330\t\n331\t        x_high, x_low = max(hull, key=x_at), min(hull, key=x_at)\n332\t        y_high, y_low = max(hull, key=y_at), min(hull, key=y_at)\n333\t        dx, dy = x_high[0] - x_low[0], x_high[1] - x_low[1]\n334\t        ex, ey = y_high[0] - y_low[0], y_high[1] - y_low[1]\n335\t\n336\t        for across, deep in orientations:\n337\t            cos_term = deep * dx - across * ey\n338\t            sin_term = -deep * dy - across * ex\n339\t            if abs(cos_term) + abs(sin_term) <= 1e-12:\n340\t                continue\n341\t            crossing = atan2(-cos_term, sin_term) % pi\n342\t            if low < crossing < high:\n343\t                candidates.append(crossing)\n344\t\n345\t    for angle in candidates:\n346\t        width, depth = _rotated_spans(hull, angle)\n347\t        if any(\n348\t            width <= across + 1e-6 and depth <= deep + 1e-6\n349\t            for across, deep in orientations\n350\t        ):\n351\t            return True\n352\t    return False\n353\t\n354\t\n355\tdef _fits_rotated(shape, bed, up=None):\n356\t    \"\"\"Whether the part fits the plate turned about some build axis.\n357\t\n358\t    The slow path, reached only after the bounding box has already failed. The\n359\t    footprint is the hull of a tessellation rather than of the B-rep's own\n360\t    vertices, because a curved outline has vertices only at its seams.\n361\t    \"\"\"\n362\t    from . import builder\n363\t\n364\t    verts = builder.to_mesh(shape, 0.1).vertices\n365\t    if up is not None:\n366\t        first, second = _bed_axes(up)\n367\t\n368\t        def projected(point):\n369\t            point = Vector(*point)\n370\t            return point.dot(first), point.dot(second)\n371\t\n372\t        footprint = [projected(point) for point in verts]\n373\t        return _footprint_fits(footprint, bed[:2])\n374\t\n375\t    for axis in range(3):\n376\t        if verts[:, axis].max() - verts[:, axis].min() > bed[2] + 1e-6:\n377\t            continue\n378\t        flat = [c for c in range(3) if c != axis]\n379\t        if _footprint_fits(verts[:, flat], bed[:2]):\n380\t            return True\n381\t    return False\n382\t\n383\t\n384\tdef _reach(face, up):\n385\t    \"\"\"How far a face protrudes, which is the smallest of its horizontal extents.\n386\t\n387\t    What makes an unsupported ledge droop is how far out it goes, not how much of it\n388\t    there is. A 0.5mm ledge prints cleanly at any length, which is why raised lettering\n389\t    is not a hundred overhang failures.\n390\t    \"\"\"\n391\t    across = [\n392\t        _span(face, axis)[1] - _span(face, axis)[0]\n393\t        for axis in (Vector(1, 0, 0), Vector(0, 1, 0), Vector(0, 0, 1))\n394\t        if abs(axis.dot(up)) < 0.9\n395\t    ]\n396\t    return min(across) if across else 0.0\n397\t\n398\t\n399\t# Height over stance width past which first-layer adhesion is holding a lever and a\n400\t# part needs fins. Provisional, sitting between a 30mm bracket on a 2mm facet that\n401\t# prints clean and a 60mm bar that does not survive without one. `stand()` grows its\n402\t# fins at the same number, so the rule and the remedy cannot drift apart.\n403\tLEVERAGE = 20\n404\t\n405\t\n406\tdef _standing(shape, up):\n407\t    \"\"\"The supported footprint width when a part stands rather than sits.\n408\t\n409\t    A part printed diagonally does not have a bottom face: it stands on a deliberate\n410\t    narrow flat, the facet `stand()` cuts across its down corner, plus the pads of\n411\t    any fins it grew. Several rules were calibrated on parts that sit, and a stance\n412\t    is the case that breaks them: there is no bottom whose rim a bed bevel could\n413\t    dress, and the centre of mass is always outside a 2mm strip.\n414\t\n415\t    Aspect ratio alone cannot identify it: a normally seated 4mm wall also has a long,\n416\t    narrow bottom. The cut facet has two long edges against sloped body faces which\n417\t    continue into other nonvertical faces; a bottom bevel instead ends at the vertical\n418\t    wall it dresses. Once found, all real bed faces count toward support, so generated\n419\t    fin pads widen the returned footprint. Returns None for a sitting part.\n420\t    \"\"\"\n421\t    FACET = 5.0\n422\t    bed, _ = _span(shape, up)\n423\t    footing = [\n424\t        f for f in shape.faces() if _span(f, up)[1] <= bed + 1e-4 and f.area >= 4.0\n425\t    ]\n426\t    if not footing:\n427\t        return None\n428\t    neighbours = None\n429\t    facet = None\n430\t    long_edge = None\n431\t    for face in footing:\n432\t        edges = list(face.edges())\n433\t        if not edges:\n434\t            continue\n435\t        longest = max(edges, key=lambda edge: edge.length)\n436\t        length = longest.length\n437\t        if length <= 0:\n438\t            continue\n439\t        width = face.area / length\n440\t        if width > FACET or length < 3 * width:\n441\t            continue\n442\t        if neighbours is None:\n443\t            neighbours = edge_faces(shape)\n444\t        stance_edges = 0\n445\t        for edge in edges:\n446\t            if edge.length < max(2 * width, 0.8 * length):\n447\t                continue\n448\t            pair = neighbours.get(edge, [])\n449\t            if len(pair) != 2:\n450\t                continue\n451\t            other = pair[1] if pair[0] == face else pair[0]\n452\t            tilt = abs(other.normal_at(edge.center()).dot(up))\n453\t            if not 1e-4 < tilt < 0.9999:\n454\t                continue\n455\t            continues = False\n456\t            for far_edge in other.edges():\n457\t                if far_edge == edge or far_edge.length < 0.8 * edge.length:\n458\t                    continue\n459\t                far_pair = neighbours.get(far_edge, [])\n460\t                if len(far_pair) != 2:\n461\t                    continue\n462\t                beyond = far_pair[1] if far_pair[0] == other else far_pair[0]\n463\t                if beyond == face:\n464\t                    continue\n465\t                beyond_tilt = abs(beyond.normal_at(far_edge.center()).dot(up))\n466\t                if 1e-4 < beyond_tilt < 0.9999:\n467\t                    continues = True\n468\t                    break\n469\t            if continues:\n470\t                stance_edges += 1\n471\t        if stance_edges >= 2:\n472\t            facet, long_edge = face, longest\n473\t            break\n474\t    if facet is None:\n475\t        return None\n476\t\n477\t    along = long_edge.tangent_at(0)\n478\t    along = (along - up * along.dot(up)).normalized()\n479\t    across = up.cross(along).normalized()\n480\t\n481\t    def footprint(axis):\n482\t        spans = [_span(face, axis) for face in footing]\n483\t        return max(high for _, high in spans) - min(low for low, _ in spans)\n484\t\n485\t    # Leverage acts across the facet, in the direction of the lean. Its length along\n486\t    # the corner already carries the first layer; fins extend support across it.\n487\t    return footprint(across)\n488\t\n489\t\n490\tdef _bridged(solid, face, up, ctx):\n491\t    \"\"\"The shortest span this face is supported across, or None if it is cantilevered.\n492\t\n493\t    A downward face with material on both sides of it is a bridge, and a printer walks\n494\t    across a short one without help. A face with material on one side only is a\n495\t    cantilever and needs support. Both are 90 degrees to the build direction and\n496\t    nothing about the normal tells them apart, which is why the overhang angle alone\n497\t    reports a channel roof and a shelf underside as the same problem.\n498\t\n499\t    Both sides get probed just outside the face and just below it, which is the same\n500\t    containment test the convexity work was checked against.\n501\t    \"\"\"\n502\t    below = face.center() - up * PROBE\n503\t    best = None\n504\t    for axis in (Vector(1, 0, 0), Vector(0, 1, 0), Vector(0, 0, 1)):\n505\t        if abs(axis.dot(up)) > 0.9:\n506\t            continue\n507\t        low, high = _span(face, axis)\n508\t        here = below.dot(axis)\n509\t        ends = [\n510\t            below + axis * (low - here - PROBE),\n511\t            below + axis * (high - here + PROBE),\n512\t        ]\n513\t        if all(solid.is_inside((p.X, p.Y, p.Z)) for p in ends):\n514\t            reach = high - low\n515\t            best = reach if best is None else min(best, reach)\n516\t    return best\n517\t\n518\t\n519\t@rule(\"overhang\")\n520\tdef overhang(shape, ctx):\n521\t    \"\"\"Downward faces the printer cannot lay down unaided.\n522\t\n523\t    Angle is measured from the build direction, so a vertical wall is 0 and a flat\n524\t    ceiling is 90. Two things are deliberately not findings: a face on the bed, which\n525\t    is the first layer, and a short bridge, which a printer spans on its own. Warning\n526\t    about either is how a checker gets switched off.\n527\t    \"\"\"\n528\t    up = Vector(*ctx.up).normalized()\n529\t    bed, _ = _span(shape, up)\n530\t    solid = shape.solids()[0] if shape.solids() else None\n531\t    found = []\n532\t    for face in shape.faces():\n533\t        if _span(face, up)[1] <= bed + 1e-4:\n534\t            continue  # grounded, so it is the first layer rather than an overhang\n535\t        worst = max(\n536\t            degrees(asin(max(-1.0, min(1.0, -n.dot(up))))) for n in _sample_normals(face)\n537\t        )\n538\t        if worst <= ctx.overhang_limit + 1e-6:\n539\t            continue\n540\t        crossing = _bridged(solid, face, up, ctx) if solid else None\n541\t        if crossing is not None and crossing <= ctx.bridge_limit:\n542\t            continue  # a bridge this printer walks across\n543\t        # A polish chamfer that a diagonal stand turns to face the bed is a strip\n544\t        # `size * 1.42` wide, the same figure concave_cosmetic uses, and it prints:\n545\t        # the flanks close over it at 45 degrees. Measured at 1.32mm on a stood\n546\t        # bracket whose flat print is clean.\n547\t        droop = max(ctx.overhang_reach, 1.42 * 1.15 * ctx.cosmetic_chamfer)\n548\t        if crossing is None and _reach(face, up) <= droop:\n549\t            continue  # a ledge too shallow to droop, whatever its angle or length\n550\t        if crossing is not None:\n551\t            found.append(\n552\t                Finding(\n553\t                    \"overhang\",\n554\t                    WARN,\n555\t                    f\"{crossing:.0f}mm bridge over {face.area:.1f}mm2, \"\n556\t                    f\"past the {ctx.bridge_limit:.0f}mm this printer spans\",\n557\t                    value=round(crossing, 1),\n558\t                    where=tuple(round(v, 2) for v in face.center()),\n559\t                )\n560\t            )\n561\t        else:\n562\t            found.append(\n563\t                Finding(\n564\t                    \"overhang\",\n565\t                    FAIL,\n566\t                    f\"{worst:.0f}deg unsupported over {face.area:.1f}mm2, \"\n567\t                    f\"limit {ctx.overhang_limit:.0f}deg\",\n568\t                    value=round(worst, 1),\n569\t                    where=tuple(round(v, 2) for v in face.center()),\n570\t                )\n571\t            )\n572\t    return found\n573\t\n574\t\n575\t@rule(\"floating\")\n576\tdef floating(shape, ctx):\n577\t    \"\"\"A region whose first layer would be laid on air.\n578\t\n579\t    The overhang rule judges faces one at a time by angle, and a floating region can\n580\t    present nothing steeper than the limit: stand an L on the end of one leg and the\n581\t    other leg's tip hangs in space with every face at exactly 45 degrees. What gives\n582\t    it away is the shape of the low point: a vertex above the bed with every edge\n583\t    rising away from it. Somewhere to sit is what a first layer needs, and angle\n584\t    cannot measure it.\n585\t\n586\t    Supported means one of two things, and both are needed. An edge running further\n587\t    down: the second-lowest corner of a stood slab has air straight below it, but its\n588\t    silhouette descends to the facet and every layer rests on the one before. Or\n589\t    material straight below: the calipers holder's corbel corner has every edge\n590\t    rising away from it and sits directly on the body underneath, which is what\n591\t    flagged the edge test as insufficient when this rule first ran on the catalog.\n592\t    The face gate mirrors the overhang rule's: a low point whose downward faces are\n593\t    all chamfer-band strips is a polish artifact, not a region.\n594\t    \"\"\"\n595\t    up = Vector(*ctx.up).normalized()\n596\t    bed, _ = _span(shape, up)\n597\t    droop = max(ctx.overhang_reach, 1.42 * 1.15 * ctx.cosmetic_chamfer)\n598\t\n599\t    def key(p):\n600\t        return (round(p.X, 4), round(p.Y, 4), round(p.Z, 4))\n601\t\n602\t    # Directions leaving each vertex, off the edge tangents rather than the chords,\n603\t    # keyed by position: the kernel hands back fresh objects on every call, so\n604\t    # identity would never match anything.\n605\t    leaving = {}\n606\t    for edge in shape.edges():\n607\t        try:\n608\t            ends = (edge.position_at(0), edge.position_at(1))\n609\t            tangents = (edge.tangent_at(0), -edge.tangent_at(1))\n610\t        except Exception:\n611\t            continue\n612\t        for point, tangent in zip(ends, tangents):\n613\t            leaving.setdefault(key(point), []).append(tangent)\n614\t    faces_at = {}\n615\t    for face in shape.faces():\n616\t        for v in face.vertices():\n617\t            faces_at.setdefault(key(Vector(v.X, v.Y, v.Z)), []).append(face)\n618\t    solid = shape.solids()[0] if shape.solids() else None\n619\t    found = []\n620\t    for spot, directions in leaving.items():\n621\t        here = Vector(*spot)\n622\t        rise = here.dot(up) - bed\n623\t        if rise <= 1e-4:\n624\t            continue  # on the bed, which is the one place a low point belongs\n625\t        if any(d.dot(up) < -1e-6 for d in directions):\n626\t            continue  # material runs further down, so this is not the low point\n627\t        below = here - up * 0.5\n628\t        if solid and solid.is_inside((below.X, below.Y, below.Z)):\n629\t            continue  # sitting on material, which is support however the edges run\n630\t        # A bead anchored on both sides at its own layer is a bridge, and a fin's\n631\t        # tine is exactly one: it starts in air and prints anyway, walked across\n632\t        # from the part to the fin. Probed a bead out each way, just above the low\n633\t        # point, so a floating tip, anchored on one side at best, stays a finding.\n634\t        level = here + up * 0.15\n635\t        horiz = [a for a in (Vector(1, 0, 0), Vector(0, 1, 0), Vector(0, 0, 1)) if abs(a.dot(up)) < 0.9]\n636\t        def held(p):\n637\t            return solid.is_inside((p.X, p.Y, p.Z))\n638\t\n639\t        if solid and any(held(level + d * 1.2) and held(level - d * 1.2) for d in horiz):\n640\t            continue\n641\t        wide = [\n642\t            f\n643\t            for f in faces_at.get(spot, [])\n644\t            if min(n.dot(up) for n in _sample_normals(f)) < -0.03 and _reach(f, up) > droop\n645\t        ]\n646\t        if not wide:\n647\t            continue\n648\t        found.append(\n649\t            Finding(\n650\t                \"floating\",\n651\t                FAIL,\n652\t                f\"a region's first layer sits on air, {rise:.1f}mm up with nothing below\",\n653\t                value=round(rise, 1),\n654\t                where=tuple(round(v, 2) for v in here),\n655\t            )\n656\t        )\n657\t    return found\n658\t\n659\t\n660\t@rule(\"hole_ceiling\")\n661\tdef hole_ceiling(shape, ctx):\n662\t    \"\"\"A blind hole's flat ceiling with a smaller hole continuing through it.\n663\t\n664\t    The counterbore case: a screw pocket printed mouth-down floats the shelf its head\n665\t    bears on, and the through hole's first rim is a circle drawn on air. The overhang\n666\t    rule cannot see it, deliberately: a ceiling with material all around reads as a\n667\t    bridge, and a bridge the printer walks across is not a finding. What a bridge\n668\t    cannot carry cleanly is a hole rim inside it, laid on sagging lines with nothing\n669\t    under the perimeter, which is why this looks for the piercing rather than the span.\n670\t\n671\t    Found on the B-rep directly: a horizontal downward face with an inner wire is a\n672\t    pierced ceiling. The one thing the wire alone cannot tell is what pierced it, so\n673\t    the probe above settles it: void continuing up is a hole and a finding; material\n674\t    is a boss hanging through, which carries its own rim. The remedy is `counterbore()`,\n675\t    which steps the transition so each layer bridges only what the one before it laid,\n676\t    and whose own stepped stack leaves no pierced ceiling for this rule to find.\n677\t    \"\"\"\n678\t    up = Vector(*ctx.up).normalized()\n679\t    bed, _ = _span(shape, up)\n680\t    solid = shape.solids()[0] if shape.solids() else None\n681\t    found = []\n682\t    for face in shape.faces():\n683\t        if face.geom_type != GeomType.PLANE:\n684\t            continue\n685\t        if face.normal_at(face.center()).dot(up) > -0.97:\n686\t            continue  # not a flat ceiling\n687\t        if _span(face, up)[0] <= bed + 1e-4:\n688\t            continue  # the first layer, where every mouth belongs\n689\t        for wire in face.inner_wires():\n690\t            # The box centre, not wire.center(): a curve's own centre is its centre\n691\t            # of mass, which for a circle is a point on the rim, not the middle.\n692\t            centre = wire.bounding_box().center()\n693\t            width = min(\n694\t                _span(wire, axis)[1] - _span(wire, axis)[0]\n695\t                for axis in (Vector(1, 0, 0), Vector(0, 1, 0), Vector(0, 0, 1))\n696\t                if abs(axis.dot(up)) < 0.9\n697\t            )\n698\t            # Probe just inside the wire rather than at its box centre. A hollow boss\n699\t            # carries the rim as surely as a solid one, but its centre is still void\n700\t            # and looks exactly like a hole. `_into_face` finds the material side of\n701\t            # the boundary, so its opposite is the region the wire encloses.\n702\t            carried = []\n703\t            if solid:\n704\t                step = min(0.05, width / 4)\n705\t                for edge in wire.edges():\n706\t                    try:\n707\t                        point = edge.position_at(0.5)\n708\t                        inward = -_into_face(face, point, edge.tangent_at(0.5))\n709\t                        above = point + inward * step + up * 0.02\n710\t                        carried.append(solid.is_inside((above.X, above.Y, above.Z)))\n711\t                    except Exception:\n712\t                        continue\n713\t            if carried and all(carried):\n714\t                continue  # a solid or hollow boss through the ceiling, not a hole\n715\t            found.append(\n716\t                Finding(\n717\t                    \"hole_ceiling\",\n718\t                    WARN,\n719\t                    f\"a {width:.1f}mm hole rim would print on air over the opening \"\n720\t                    f\"below; stepped bridging layers are the fix\",\n721\t                    value=round(width, 1),\n722\t                    where=tuple(round(v, 2) for v in centre),\n723\t                )\n724\t            )\n725\t    return found\n726\t\n727\t\n728\t@rule(\"stability\")\n729\tdef stability(shape, ctx):\n730\t    \"\"\"Will it stand on the bed, or tip while printing.\"\"\"\n731\t    up = Vector(*ctx.up).normalized()\n732\t    bed, top = _span(shape, up)\n733\t    footing = [f for f in shape.faces() if _span(f, up)[1] <= bed + 1e-4]\n734\t    if not footing:\n735\t        return [Finding(\"stability\", FAIL, \"nothing flat to stand on\")]\n736\t    stance = _standing(shape, up)\n737\t    if stance is not None:\n738\t        # Standing on a facet, the centre of mass is always outside it and the part\n739\t        # is held by first-layer adhesion, not balance. What adhesion cannot hold is\n740\t        # leverage; fins widen the stance, which is how growing them clears this.\n741\t        if top - bed > LEVERAGE * stance:\n742\t            return [\n743\t                Finding(\n744\t                    \"stability\",\n745\t                    WARN,\n746\t                    f\"stands {top - bed:.0f}mm tall on a {stance:.1f}mm facet, \"\n747\t                    \"more than adhesion holds; a support fin is the fix\",\n748\t                    value=round((top - bed) / stance, 1),\n749\t                )\n750\t            ]\n751\t        return []\n752\t    com = shape.center(CenterOf.MASS)\n753\t    axes = [a for a in (Vector(1, 0, 0), Vector(0, 1, 0), Vector(0, 0, 1)) if abs(a.dot(up)) < 0.9]\n754\t    for axis in axes:\n755\t        reach = [end for f in footing for end in _span(f, axis)]\n756\t        here = com.dot(axis)\n757\t        if not min(reach) <= here <= max(reach):\n758\t            return [\n759\t                Finding(\n760\t                    \"stability\",\n761\t                    WARN,\n762\t                    \"center of mass falls outside the footprint, it will tip\",\n763\t                    where=tuple(round(v, 2) for v in com),\n764\t                )\n765\t            ]\n766\t    return []\n767\t\n768\t\n769\t# --- printer profiles --------------------------------------------------------\n770\t\n771\tPRINTERS = pathlib.Path(__file__).parent / \"printers.toml\"\n772\tPRINTER_FILE = \"printer.toml\"  # optional, at the project root, like measurements.toml\n773\t\n774\t\n775\tdef global_file():\n776\t    \"\"\"~/.config/nurb/config.toml, the machine's standing answers.\n777\t\n778\t    A printer is a fact about the workshop, not the project, so it is named once\n779\t    here instead of in every printer.toml. Same schema as printer.toml, read fresh\n780\t    each call because tests move it with XDG_CONFIG_HOME.\n781\t    \"\"\"\n782\t    import os\n783\t\n784\t    base = os.environ.get(\"XDG_CONFIG_HOME\") or pathlib.Path.home() / \".config\"\n785\t    return pathlib.Path(base) / \"nurb\" / \"config.toml\"\n786\t\n787\t\n788\tdef _read_toml(file, label):\n789\t    import tomllib\n790\t\n791\t    if not file.is_file():\n792\t        return {}\n793\t    try:\n794\t        return tomllib.loads(file.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n795\t    except tomllib.TOMLDecodeError as exc:\n796\t        raise ValueError(f\"{label}: not valid TOML ({exc})\") from exc\n797\t\n798\t\n799\tdef profile_choice(root):\n800\t    \"\"\"The profile a project resolves to and where it was named: the project's\n801\t    printer.toml, then the global config. (None, None) when neither says.\"\"\"\n802\t    block = _read_toml(pathlib.Path(root) / PRINTER_FILE, PRINTER_FILE)\n803\t    if block.get(\"profile\"):\n804\t        return block[\"profile\"], PRINTER_FILE\n805\t    home = _read_toml(global_file(), str(global_file()))\n806\t    if home.get(\"profile\"):\n807\t        return home[\"profile\"], \"global\"\n808\t    return None, None\n809\t\n810\t\n811\tdef profiles():\n812\t    \"\"\"The shipped printer profiles: machine facts, keyed by name.\"\"\"\n813\t    import tomllib\n814\t\n815\t    return tomllib.loads(PRINTERS.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n816\t\n817\t\n818\tdef _project_root(part_path):\n819\t    \"\"\"The project a part belongs to, by the same rule the builder uses.\"\"\"\n820\t    path = pathlib.Path(part_path).resolve()\n821\t    return path.parent.parent if path.parent.name == \"parts\" else path.parent\n822\t\n823\t\n824\tdef printer(root, name=None):\n825\t    \"\"\"The machine's Context: a shipped profile, then the global config, then the\n826\t    project's printer.toml.\n827\t\n828\t    A bed size belongs to the machine, not to a part, so it is picked once here\n829\t    rather than written onto every card. The doctrine's corbel is a 45 degree underside, and\n990\t    where it lands on the plate rather than dying into the body it is exactly such a\n991\t    face: structure, prescribed, and 46mm2 of it.\n992\t\n993\t    What separates them is how far the face rises off the bed, and a bottom chamfer's\n994\t    rise is its size exactly. Measured: 1.00, 2.00 and 3.00mm for chamfers of those\n995\t    sizes, against 4.29mm for the corbel. Rise is independent of the edge's path: a 1mm\n996\t    chamfer around a 20mm cylinder is still 1mm high even though its plan-view bounding\n997\t    box is 20mm in both directions. The limit is three times the polish size because the\n998\t    largest chamfer this doctrine puts anywhere is the 3mm structural relief, and the\n999\t    smallest corbel starts with a 4 to 6mm vertical tip.\n1000\t    \"\"\"\n1001\t    up = Vector(*ctx.up).normalized()\n1002\t    bed, _ = _span(shape, up)\n1003\t    if _standing(shape, up) is not None:\n1004\t        # Standing on a facet, so every face at the plate is deliberately tilted and\n1005\t        # there is no bottom whose rim a bevel could dress. A 4mm wall stood at 45\n1006\t        # rises 2.83mm, square in the chamfer band, and prints fine: rise cannot tell\n1007\t        # it from a bevel, but the stance can.\n1008\t        return []\n1009\t    found = []\n1010\t    for face in shape.faces():\n1011\t        low, high = _span(face, up)\n1012\t        if low > bed + 1e-4:\n1013\t            continue  # does not reach the plate\n1014\t        if high - low > 3 * ctx.cosmetic_chamfer:\n1015\t            continue  # rises too far to be a chamfer band, so it is structure\n1016\t        for normal in _sample_normals(face):\n1017\t            tilt = abs(normal.dot(up))\n1018\t            if 0.03 < tilt < 0.97:  # neither lying on the plate nor standing on it\n1019\t                found.append(\n1020\t                    Finding(\n1021\t                        \"bed_bevel\",\n1022\t                        WARN,\n1023\t                        f\"{degrees(asin(min(1.0, tilt))):.0f}deg face meets the build \"\n1024\t                        f\"plate over {face.area:.1f}mm2, which is polish on the first layer\",\n1025\t                        value=round(face.area, 2),\n1026\t                        where=tuple(round(v, 2) for v in face.center()),\n1027\t                    )\n1028\t                )\n1029\t                break\n1030\t    return found\n1031\t\n1032\t\n1033\t@rule(\"concave_cosmetic\")\n1034\tdef concave_cosmetic(shape, ctx):\n1035\t    \"\"\"Polish laid into an inside corner instead of across an outside one.\n1036\t\n1037\t    A cosmetic chamfer belongs on a convex edge, where it takes a sharp corner off. Run\n1038\t    over a concave one it does the opposite: it adds a thin wedge into the corner,\n1039\t    which prints as a feather edge and collects stress where the part is already\n1040\t    weakest. A concave junction that needs relieving wants a deliberate structural\n1041\t    chamfer sized for the load, not the polish pass.\n1042\t\n1043\t    Found by shape rather than by trying to identify chamfers: a strip about as wide as\n1044\t    the polish pass makes, whose long edges are all concave, is one. Width comes from\n1045\t    area over perimeter, which is the honest measure for a strip and does not care how\n1046\t    the strip is oriented.\n1047\t\n1048\t    **About as wide as the polish pass makes, not merely narrow.** A chamfer of length s\n1049\t    across a right-angled corner leaves a strip s * sqrt(2) wide, so the window is that\n1050\t    figure with a little headroom, and the limit was twice it until two parts showed\n1051\t    what twice costs. A 2mm structural chamfer, which is what the doctrine prescribes\n1052\t    for a concave junction in thin material, leaves a 2.6mm strip; the 2mm of wall left\n1053\t    standing between two of them measures 1.9mm. Both sat under a 2.8mm limit, so the\n1054\t    rule fired six times at `mount_tape_measure` and again at `mount_akrobin_rail`, at\n1055\t    the exact geometry its own message tells you to use. A deliberate relief is bigger\n1056\t    than polish, and being bigger is the only thing that distinguishes it.\n1057\t\n1058\t    The cost is a polish chamfer in a shallow concave junction, past about 105 degrees,\n1059\t    where the strip a 1mm chamfer leaves is wide enough to read as deliberate. That is\n1060\t    the right way round to be wrong: `polish_edges` vetoes concave edges outright, so\n1061\t    this rule is the backstop for a part that rolls its own selector, and a backstop\n1062\t    that cries wolf at correct geometry gets switched off.\n1063\t    \"\"\"\n1064\t    neighbours = edge_faces(shape)\n1065\t    limit = ctx.cosmetic_chamfer * 1.42 * 1.15\n1066\t    found = []\n1067\t    for face in shape.faces():\n1068\t        if face.geom_type != GeomType.PLANE:\n1069\t            continue\n1070\t        perimeter = sum(e.length for e in face.edges())\n1071\t        if perimeter <= 0:\n1072\t            continue\n1073\t        width = 2 * face.area / perimeter\n1074\t        if width > limit:\n1075\t            continue\n1076\t        long_edges = [e for e in face.edges() if e.length > width * 1.5]\n1077\t        if len(long_edges) < 2:\n1078\t            continue\n1079\t        here = face.normal_at(face.center())\n1080\t        verdicts, oblique = [], []\n1081\t        for edge in long_edges:\n1082\t            pair = neighbours.get(edge, [])\n1083\t            if len(pair) != 2:\n1084\t                continue\n1085\t            verdicts.append(is_convex(edge, *pair))\n1086\t            # Compared by topology, not by `is`: shape.faces() hands back fresh\n1087\t            # objects each call, so identity would always pick this face itself and\n1088\t            # every junction would look oblique.\n1089\t            other = pair[1] if pair[0] == face else pair[0]\n1090\t            # A bevel leans against what it joins. A pocket floor is square to its\n1091\t            # walls, and a pocket is not polish however narrow it is.\n1092\t            oblique.append(abs(here.dot(other.normal_at(edge.center()))) > 0.3)\n1093\t        if verdicts and not any(verdicts) and oblique and all(oblique):\n1094\t            found.append(\n1095\t                Finding(\n1096\t                    \"concave_cosmetic\",\n1097\t                    WARN,\n1098\t                    f\"{width:.1f}mm strip sitting in a concave junction over \"\n1099\t                    f\"{face.area:.1f}mm2, which is polish where a structural chamfer belongs\",\n1100\t                    value=round(width, 2),\n1101\t                    where=tuple(round(v, 2) for v in face.center()),\n1102\t                )\n1103\t            )\n1104\t    return found\n1105\t\n1106\t\n1107\tdef _probes(face, grid=2):\n1108\t    \"\"\"Points across a face with the outward normal at each.\"\"\"\n1109\t    spots = [face.center()]\n1110\t    if face.geom_type != GeomType.PLANE or face.area > 25:\n1111\t        # Inset from the boundary on purpose. A probe sitting on a face's own edge\n1112\t        # measures the distance to whatever is around the corner, which is not a wall.\n1113\t        for i in range(grid):\n1114\t            for j in range(grid):\n1115\t                try:\n1116\t                    spots.append(\n1117\t                        face.position_at((i + 1) / (grid + 1), (j + 1) / (grid + 1))\n1118\t                    )\n1119\t                except Exception:\n1120\t                    continue\n1121\t    out = []\n1122\t    for spot in spots:\n1123\t        try:\n1124\t            out.append((spot, face.normal_at(spot)))\n1125\t        except Exception:\n1126\t            continue\n1127\t    return out\n1128\t\n1129\t\n1130\tdef _sphere_at(shape, point, outward, chord):\n1131\t    \"\"\"The largest sphere tangent to the solid at `point`, as a diameter, or None.\n1132\t\n1133\t    The shrinking ball: start at half the ray's chord, ask the kernel for the nearest\n1134\t    boundary point to the center, and where something is nearer than the radius, shrink\n1135\t    to the sphere through it. Distances are exact B-rep queries, so this stays a check\n1136\t    on the solid rather than on a mesh, and it converges in a handful of steps because\n1137\t    the tangency update jumps straight to the answer rather than bisecting toward it.\n1138\t\n1139\t    None means the far contact was a graze rather than a wall. The contact normal comes\n1140\t    free, as (q - c) / r, and it gets the same 0.3 floor the ray's exit filter uses,\n1141\t    with the same meaning: a measurement has to leave through a surface facing back at\n1142\t    it. Without this, a shallow depression reads as a thin section of whatever it is\n1143\t    pressed into: the scraper's detent dimple measured 1.07mm sitting in a 2.3mm web,\n1144\t    between two surfaces bounding the same air.\n1145\t    \"\"\"\n1146\t    inward = -outward\n1147\t    r = chord / 2\n1148\t    w = None\n1149\t    for _ in range(12):\n1150\t        center = point + inward * r\n1151\t        d, q, _ = shape.distance_to_with_closest_points(center)\n1152\t        if d >= r - 1e-5:\n1153\t            break\n1154\t        w = point - q\n1155\t        if w.length < 1e-6:\n1156\t            return None  # pinned at the tangent point, which is curvature, not a wall\n1157\t        denom = 2 * w.dot(outward)\n1158\t        if denom <= 1e-9:\n1159\t            return None  # the contact is behind the tangent plane, nothing to measure\n1160\t        grown = w.dot(w) / denom\n1161\t        if grown >= r - 1e-6:\n1162\t            break\n1163\t        r = grown\n1164\t    if w is not None and w.dot(w) / (2 * r * r) - 1 <= 0.3:\n1165\t        return None\n1166\t    return 2 * r\n1167\t\n1168\t\n1169\t@rule(\"min_wall\")\n1170\tdef min_wall(shape, ctx):\n1171\t    \"\"\"The thinnest section in the solid: a ray cast, corrected by an inscribed sphere\n1172\t    wherever the correction could change the verdict.\n1173\t\n1174\t    The ray is exact on the flat parallel walls that make up most of a printed part,\n1175\t    and an overestimate everywhere else: through a skewed wall it measures the slant,\n1176\t    up to 1/0.3 of the true section at its own exit filter's floor. So any chord thin\n1177\t    enough that the true section might still be under `min_wall` gets refined by\n1178\t    `_sphere_at`, which measures the way a wall is actually thin, perpendicular to\n1179\t    nothing in particular. The gate follows from the filter rather than being tuned: an\n1180\t    accepted chord leaves within the 0.3 cosine floor, so a chord past `min_wall / 0.3`\n1181\t    cannot be hiding a failing section, and the sphere is only paid for where it could\n1182\t    matter. That also prices `min_wall = 0`, the card's way of saying \"this rule cannot\n1183\t    measure this part\", at nothing.\n1184\t\n1185\t    Two caveats survive the correction, and live on cards rather than in code:\n1186\t\n1187\t    - A taper reads as a wall. Near the tip of a designed knife edge every section is\n1188\t      short, sphere and chord alike. The gridfinity shelves' sockets measure 0.84mm\n1189\t      where their 45 degree walls meet, and their cards allow 0.7 next to the sentence\n1190\t      saying why.\n1191\t    - A relief reads as a wall. The calibration coupon's raised label is 0.5mm proud,\n1192\t      and that is what gets measured, so its card sets `min_wall = 0`.\n1193\t\n1194\t    Probes sample faces, so a pinch nothing lands near is still missed. A hit facing back at the ray means the\n1207\t            # ray had already left the material and struck something across a gap,\n1208\t            # which is how a chamfer two corners away reads as a thin wall.\n1209\t            reach = [\n1210\t                (hit - point).length\n1211\t                for hit, hit_normal in hits\n1212\t                if (hit - point).dot(inward) > floor\n1213\t                and hit_normal.dot(inward) > 0.3\n1214\t            ]\n1215\t            if not reach:\n1216\t                continue\n1217\t            near = min(reach)\n1218\t            if near * 0.3 < ctx.min_wall:\n1219\t                # The sphere starts from the first boundary crossing rather than from\n1220\t                # the accepted chord, because half of that is guaranteed to sit inside\n1221\t                # the material, and the shrink only ever moves down from there.\n1222\t                first = min(\n1223\t                    (hit - point).length for hit, _ in hits if (hit - point).length > floor\n1224\t                )\n1225\t                section = _sphere_at(shape, point, normal, first)\n1226\t                if section is not None and floor < section < near:\n1227\t                    near = section\n1228\t            if thinnest is None or near < thinnest:\n1229\t                thinnest, spot = near, point\n1230\t    # Slack, because a card declaring the value it measured should not then fail on the\n1231\t    # last bit of a float. One percent rather than a micron: the same solid measures\n1232\t    # 0.500 against one OCCT build and 0.498 against another, and a threshold tight\n1233\t    # enough to see that turns a card into a claim about which machine wrote it. A card\n1234\t    # can also set `min_wall = 0` to say the rule cannot measure this part at all, which\n1235\t    # is honest for anything whose thinnest apparent section is not a wall.\n1236\t    if thinnest is None or thinnest >= ctx.min_wall * 0.99 - 1e-3:\n1237\t        return []\n1238\t    return [\n1239\t        Finding(\n1240\t            \"min_wall\",\n1241\t            WARN,\n1242\t            f\"{thinnest:.2f}mm section, under the {ctx.min_wall:.2f}mm this printer \"\n1243\t            f\"lays down reliably\",\n1244\t            value=round(thinnest, 3),\n1245\t            where=tuple(round(v, 2) for v in spot),\n1246\t        )\n1247\t    ]\n1248\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"d0e609ba-d92b-4426-ad5b-d572881ae252","uuid":"8af56515-dce5-44c0-be07-4aa0d42d2af8","timestamp":"2026-08-03T20:04:08.652Z","tool_use_result":{"type":"text","file":{"filePath":"<home>/Development/nurb/src/nurb/checks.py","content":"\"\"\"Printability rules, run against the solid rather than against an export.\n\nChecking the B-rep instead of an STL means real faces with exact areas and normals\ninstead of triangles, which is what makes most of these rules cheap and exact.\n\nA rule takes the shape and a Context and yields Findings. Rules compose: they never\nsee each other, and `run` gathers them.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport pathlib\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field, replace\nfrom math import asin, atan2, cos, degrees, pi, sin\n\nfrom build123d import Axis, CenterOf, GeomType, Vector\n\nFAIL = \"fail\"  # this will not print, or will not work\nWARN = \"warn\"  # this needs attention, most often support\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Finding:\n    rule: str\n    severity: str\n    message: str\n    value: float | None = None\n    where: tuple | None = None\n\n    def __str__(self):\n        spot = f\"  at ({self.where[0]:.1f}, {self.where[1]:.1f}, {self.where[2]:.1f})\" if self.where else \"\"\n        return f\"{self.severity:4}  {self.rule:20} {self.message}{spot}\"\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass Context:\n    \"\"\"What a rule needs to know beyond the geometry itself.\n\n    `up` is the build direction, which is the model's z only when a part is printed\n    the way it is modelled. Notch parts are, so +z is right for them. Nothing\n    guarantees that in general, and getting it wrong does not error: it reports\n    confident nonsense about every face in the part.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    bed: tuple = (256.0, 256.0, 256.0)\n    up: tuple = (0.0, 0.0, 1.0)\n    overhang_limit: float = 45.0  # degrees away from the build direction\n    bridge_limit: float = 30.0  # how far this printer will span unsupported\n    overhang_reach: float = 1.0  # a ledge shorter than this cannot droop enough to matter\n    forward: tuple | None = None  # which way a wall-mounted part cantilevers, if it does\n    projection_limit: float = 2.5  # reach over height, past which height is the fix\n    cosmetic_chamfer: float = 1.0  # the size the polish pass uses\n    min_wall: float = 1.0  # what this printer lays down reliably; per part, per card\n    sliver_area: float = 1.0\n    accepted: dict = field(default_factory=dict)  # rule -> how many are already known\n\n\nRULES = {}\n\n\ndef rule(name):\n    def register(fn):\n        RULES[name] = fn\n        return fn\n\n    return register\n\n\ndef run(shape, ctx=None, only=None):\n    # An assembly's compound carries its recorded joints, and motion is the only\n    # thing worth judging about it: overhang and min_wall on an assembled scene\n    # would report confident nonsense about parts that each checked clean alone.\n    scene = getattr(shape, \"_nurb_scene\", None)\n    if scene is not None:\n        from .assembly import sweep\n\n        return sweep(scene)\n    ctx = ctx or Context()\n    found = []\n    for name, fn in RULES.items():\n        if only and name not in only:\n            continue\n        found.extend(fn(shape, ctx))\n    return sorted(found, key=lambda f: (f.severity != FAIL, f.rule))\n\n\n# --- geometry helpers --------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef edge_faces(shape):\n    \"\"\"Every edge mapped to the faces sharing it.\"\"\"\n    out = {}\n    for face in shape.faces():\n        for edge in face.edges():\n            out.setdefault(edge, []).append(face)\n    return out\n\n\nPROBE = 1e-3\n\n\ndef _into_face(face, point, tangent):\n    \"\"\"The direction leaving `point` across the face, perpendicular to the edge.\n\n    Which of the two candidates is the right one gets settled by stepping and asking\n    the face, rather than by trusting a winding convention. Aiming at the centroid\n    instead is tempting and wrong: a face that is L-shaped or has the rest of the part\n    merged into it puts its centroid somewhere that has nothing to do with which side\n    of this edge the material is on. That mistake showed up as convex slab edges\n    reported concave.\n    \"\"\"\n    across = tangent.cross(face.normal_at(point)).normalized()\n    forward = face.distance_to(point + across * PROBE)\n    backward = face.distance_to(point - across * PROBE)\n    return across if forward <= backward else -across\n\n\ndef is_convex(edge, f1, f2):\n    \"\"\"True if the material folds away from itself across this edge.\n\n    The test is `n1 . u2`: does the second face extend in the direction the first\n    face's outward normal points? If it does, the solid is folding back on itself and\n    the edge is concave.\n\n    The obvious alternative, offsetting the edge along the averaged face normals and\n    asking whether that point is inside the solid, is recorded in the Fusion notes as\n    failing. It cannot work: at a concave edge the averaged normal points into open\n    space just as it does at a convex one, so both answer the same. What separates\n    them is where the faces go, not where they face, which is why this uses a\n    direction along the face and not the normal.\n    \"\"\"\n    point = edge.center()\n    tangent = edge.tangent_at(0.5)\n    return f1.normal_at(point).dot(_into_face(f2, point, tangent)) < 0\n\n\ndef concave_edges(shape):\n    \"\"\"Every concave edge, which is where polish is forbidden and stress collects.\"\"\"\n    out = []\n    for edge, faces in edge_faces(shape).items():\n        if len(faces) != 2:\n            continue  # seam or free edge, nothing to be convex about\n        if not is_convex(edge, faces[0], faces[1]):\n            out.append(edge)\n    return out\n\n\n# --- rules -------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@rule(\"sliver\")\ndef sliver(shape, ctx):\n    \"\"\"Faces too small to print as anything but a smear.\n\n    Chamfers meeting at a corner legitimately make a few, so a part declares how many\n    it has earned. A count above the baseline is a regression; at or below it is\n    silence. The count is the assertion, not the individual faces, because which face\n    is which shifts as soon as anything upstream of the polish pass moves.\n    \"\"\"\n    small = [f for f in shape.faces() if f.area < ctx.sliver_area]\n    allowed = ctx.accepted.get(\"sliver\", 0)\n    if len(small) <= allowed:\n        return []\n    worst = min(small, key=lambda f: f.area)\n    return [\n        Finding(\n            \"sliver\",\n            WARN,\n            f\"{len(small)} faces under {ctx.sliver_area}mm2, {allowed} accounted for\",\n            value=round(worst.area, 3),\n            where=tuple(round(v, 2) for v in worst.center()),\n        )\n    ]\n\n\n@rule(\"build_volume\")\ndef build_volume(shape, ctx):\n    \"\"\"Does it fit on the printer at all.\"\"\"\n    size = shape.bounding_box().size\n    up = Vector(*ctx.up).normalized()\n    if _standing(shape, up) is None:\n        got = sorted([size.X, size.Y, size.Z], reverse=True)\n        fits = all(g <= b for g, b in zip(got, sorted(ctx.bed, reverse=True)))\n        if not fits:\n            # The box is the wrong question for a part that fits turned on the\n            # plate: issue #55's 364mm tray sits on a 350mm bed at 36 degrees.\n            fits = _fits_rotated(shape, ctx.bed)\n        orientation = \"in any orientation\"\n    else:\n        # A stance facet fixes the build direction. The part may turn on the plate,\n        # but rolling it onto another axis would put the facet in the air again.\n        bed, top = _span(shape, up)\n        height = top - bed\n        footprint = sorted(\n            _span(shape, axis)[1] - _span(shape, axis)[0]\n            for axis in _bed_axes(up)\n        )\n        plate = sorted(ctx.bed[:2])\n        got = [height, *footprint]\n        fits = height <= ctx.bed[2] and (\n            all(got <= available for got, available in zip(footprint, plate))\n            or _fits_rotated(shape, ctx.bed, up)\n        )\n        orientation = \"in its fixed build orientation\"\n    if fits:\n        return []\n    return [\n        Finding(\n            \"build_volume\",\n            FAIL,\n            f\"{size.X:.0f} x {size.Y:.0f} x {size.Z:.0f}mm does not fit \"\n            f\"{ctx.bed[0]:.0f} x {ctx.bed[1]:.0f} x {ctx.bed[2]:.0f}mm {orientation}\",\n            value=round(max(got), 1),\n        )\n    ]\n\n\ndef _sample_normals(face, grid=5):\n    \"\"\"Outward normals across a face.\n\n    A planar face has one and it is exact. A curved face does not, and the sampling\n    has to reach the ends of its parameter range: sampling cell midpoints instead\n    walks a cylinder round at 45, 135, 225 and 315 degrees and never once looks\n    straight down, which is the only normal an overhang check cares about. Endpoints\n    included, the same grid hits 270 exactly.\n\n    Still an approximation on a curved face, bounded by the grid spacing. Planar\n    faces, which is nearly all of this library, are exact.\n    \"\"\"\n    if face.geom_type == GeomType.PLANE:\n        return [face.normal_at(face.center())]\n    out = []\n    for i in range(grid):\n        for j in range(grid):\n            try:\n                spot = face.position_at(i / (grid - 1), j / (grid - 1))\n                out.append(face.normal_at(spot))\n            except Exception:\n                continue\n    return out or [face.normal_at(face.center())]\n\n\ndef _span(shape, up):\n    \"\"\"How far a shape reaches along the build direction, as (lowest, highest).\n\n    Off the bounding box, not off the vertices. A curved face has vertices only where\n    its seam is, so a cylinder's lowest vertex sits at its middle: measuring from\n    vertices put the bed 8mm above the actual bottom of a cylinder, marked every face\n    grounded, and turned the overhang rule off without any sign that it had happened.\n\n    Exact for an axis-aligned build direction, which is what a part placed on a bed\n    has. Conservative otherwise.\n    \"\"\"\n    box = shape.bounding_box()\n    corners = [\n        Vector(x, y, z)\n        for x in (box.min.X, box.max.X)\n        for y in (box.min.Y, box.max.Y)\n        for z in (box.min.Z, box.max.Z)\n    ]\n    reach = [c.dot(up) for c in corners]\n    return min(reach), max(reach)\n\n\ndef _bed_axes(up):\n    \"\"\"Two perpendicular directions across the build plate.\"\"\"\n    guide = Vector(1, 0, 0) if abs(up.X) < 0.9 else Vector(0, 1, 0)\n    first = up.cross(guide).normalized()\n    return first, up.cross(first).normalized()\n\n\ndef _hull(points):\n    \"\"\"Convex hull of 2d points, by monotone chain.\"\"\"\n    pts = sorted({(round(float(x), 2), round(float(y), 2)) for x, y in points})\n    if len(pts) <= 2:\n        return pts\n\n    def turn(o, a, b):\n        return (a[0] - o[0]) * (b[1] - o[1]) - (a[1] - o[1]) * (b[0] - o[0])\n\n    out = []\n    for chain in (pts, reversed(pts)):\n        base = len(out)\n        for p in chain:\n            while len(out) - base >= 2 and turn(out[-2], out[-1], p) <= 0:\n                out.pop()\n            out.append(p)\n        out.pop()\n    return out\n\n\ndef _rotated_spans(points, angle):\n    c, s = cos(angle), sin(angle)\n    xs = [x * c - y * s for x, y in points]\n    ys = [x * s + y * c for x, y in points]\n    return max(xs) - min(xs), max(ys) - min(ys)\n\n\ndef _footprint_fits(points, plate):\n    \"\"\"Whether a 2d footprint fits a rectangular plate at some rotation.\n\n    Between orientations parallel to hull edges, the vertices supporting each side\n    of the bounding box stay fixed. The worst normalized span can reach its minimum\n    only at one of those boundaries or where the two normalized spans cross, so those\n    finite candidates give the exact answer without an angle grid.\n    \"\"\"\n    hull = _hull(points)\n    if not hull:\n        return False\n\n    quarter = pi / 2\n    cuts = {0.0, quarter}\n    if len(hull) > 1:\n        for p, q in zip(hull, hull[1:] + hull[:1]):\n            edge = atan2(q[1] - p[1], q[0] - p[0])\n            cuts.add((-edge) % quarter)\n    cuts = sorted(cuts)\n    candidates = list(cuts)\n    orientations = (plate, plate[::-1])\n\n    for low, high in zip(cuts, cuts[1:]):\n        if high - low <= 1e-12:\n            continue\n        mid = (low + high) / 2\n        c, s = cos(mid), sin(mid)\n\n        def x_at(p):\n            return p[0] * c - p[1] * s\n\n        def y_at(p):\n            return p[0] * s + p[1] * c\n\n        x_high, x_low = max(hull, key=x_at), min(hull, key=x_at)\n        y_high, y_low = max(hull, key=y_at), min(hull, key=y_at)\n        dx, dy = x_high[0] - x_low[0], x_high[1] - x_low[1]\n        ex, ey = y_high[0] - y_low[0], y_high[1] - y_low[1]\n\n        for across, deep in orientations:\n            cos_term = deep * dx - across * ey\n            sin_term = -deep * dy - across * ex\n            if abs(cos_term) + abs(sin_term) <= 1e-12:\n                continue\n            crossing = atan2(-cos_term, sin_term) % pi\n            if low < crossing < high:\n                candidates.append(crossing)\n\n    for angle in candidates:\n        width, depth = _rotated_spans(hull, angle)\n        if any(\n            width <= across + 1e-6 and depth <= deep + 1e-6\n            for across, deep in orientations\n        ):\n            return True\n    return False\n\n\ndef _fits_rotated(shape, bed, up=None):\n    \"\"\"Whether the part fits the plate turned about some build axis.\n\n    The slow path, reached only after the bounding box has already failed. The\n    footprint is the hull of a tessellation rather than of the B-rep's own\n    vertices, because a curved outline has vertices only at its seams.\n    \"\"\"\n    from . import builder\n\n    verts = builder.to_mesh(shape, 0.1).vertices\n    if up is not None:\n        first, second = _bed_axes(up)\n\n        def projected(point):\n            point = Vector(*point)\n            return point.dot(first), point.dot(second)\n\n        footprint = [projected(point) for point in verts]\n        return _footprint_fits(footprint, bed[:2])\n\n    for axis in range(3):\n        if verts[:, axis].max() - verts[:, axis].min() > bed[2] + 1e-6:\n            continue\n        flat = [c for c in range(3) if c != axis]\n        if _footprint_fits(verts[:, flat], bed[:2]):\n            return True\n    return False\n\n\ndef _reach(face, up):\n    \"\"\"How far a face protrudes, which is the smallest of its horizontal extents.\n\n    What makes an unsupported ledge droop is how far out it goes, not how much of it\n    there is. A 0.5mm ledge prints cleanly at any length, which is why raised lettering\n    is not a hundred overhang failures.\n    \"\"\"\n    across = [\n        _span(face, axis)[1] - _span(face, axis)[0]\n        for axis in (Vector(1, 0, 0), Vector(0, 1, 0), Vector(0, 0, 1))\n        if abs(axis.dot(up)) < 0.9\n    ]\n    return min(across) if across else 0.0\n\n\n# Height over stance width past which first-layer adhesion is holding a lever and a\n# part needs fins. Provisional, sitting between a 30mm bracket on a 2mm facet that\n# prints clean and a 60mm bar that does not survive without one. `stand()` grows its\n# fins at the same number, so the rule and the remedy cannot drift apart.\nLEVERAGE = 20\n\n\ndef _standing(shape, up):\n    \"\"\"The supported footprint width when a part stands rather than sits.\n\n    A part printed diagonally does not have a bottom face: it stands on a deliberate\n    narrow flat, the facet `stand()` cuts across its down corner, plus the pads of\n    any fins it grew. Several rules were calibrated on parts that sit, and a stance\n    is the case that breaks them: there is no bottom whose rim a bed bevel could\n    dress, and the centre of mass is always outside a 2mm strip.\n\n    Aspect ratio alone cannot identify it: a normally seated 4mm wall also has a long,\n    narrow bottom. The cut facet has two long edges against sloped body faces which\n    continue into other nonvertical faces; a bottom bevel instead ends at the vertical\n    wall it dresses. Once found, all real bed faces count toward support, so generated\n    fin pads widen the returned footprint. Returns None for a sitting part.\n    \"\"\"\n    FACET = 5.0\n    bed, _ = _span(shape, up)\n    footing = [\n        f for f in shape.faces() if _span(f, up)[1] <= bed + 1e-4 and f.area >= 4.0\n    ]\n    if not footing:\n        return None\n    neighbours = None\n    facet = None\n    long_edge = None\n    for face in footing:\n        edges = list(face.edges())\n        if not edges:\n            continue\n        longest = max(edges, key=lambda edge: edge.length)\n        length = longest.length\n        if length <= 0:\n            continue\n        width = face.area / length\n        if width > FACET or length < 3 * width:\n            continue\n        if neighbours is None:\n            neighbours = edge_faces(shape)\n        stance_edges = 0\n        for edge in edges:\n
